_source line 21491 · 2026-05-22T11:03:31.097Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a Paper Agent session for the "symb_invariant_consensus" EMNLP 2026 paper about Symbolic Confirmation Bias (SCB) in LLM self-verification. The session handled multiple sequential Director instructions in Patch mode:

   **A. Discussion expansion (carried over, completed):** Expand Discussion from 1 paragraph to 3 structured paragraphs + expand appendix Extended Discussion + maintain 8-page main text limit.

   **B. Related Work deepening + Appendix cleanup (completed):** Check Related Work coverage of self-verification limitations, ensemble diversity, formal verification. Add missing citations. Check appendix numbering, cross-references, and dedup with main Discussion.

   **C. Page 8 layout optimization (completed):** Move DRC paragraph to appendix, reduce `\enlargethispage` from 12→4-6, restore Discussion heading spacing. Goal: page 8 visual density comparable to other pages.

   **D. Multi-seed data integration - Qwen2.5 × FOLIO (IN PROGRESS):** Add multi-seed table to appendix, add main text sentence, update Table 1 footnote, update Limitations. Data: 4 seeds, all Δ ≤ 0, mean -1.24±0.82pp.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026: 8-page strict main text limit; Limitations/References/Appendix unlimited
   - Symbolic Confirmation Bias (SCB): BR 1.66×-4.97× across nine models
   - Four diagnostics: K_eff (effective vote diversity), BR (bias ratio), C₀ (degenerate consensus), ProcessSim
   - Three escape conditions: structural independence, positive capability gap, task-verifier alignment
   - `\enlargethispage{N\baselineskip}` for page extension — currently at 6\baselineskip
   - Appendix section counter limit: 26 sections (A-Z), currently at exactly 26
   - `$^{4s}

 notation in Table 1 for 4-seed confirmatory conditions
   - Paper Agent uses `paper_agent_post_to_director` (NOT `main_agent_post_to_director`)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **discussion.tex** — Main Discussion section (§7). Final state:
     ```latex
     \enlargethispage{6\baselineskip}
     \section{Discussion and Conclusion}
     \label{sec:discussion}
     \label{sec:conclusion}
     
     \paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
     \looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$: no condition improves ($\Delta \in [0.00, -0.98]$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:independence}). SC $< 40\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\Delta = -2.83$\,pp). $C_0 < 5\%$: near-unanimous traces make aggregation equivalent to majority voting. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.
     
     \paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
     \looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.
     
     \paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
     \looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\kappa \geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.
     ```
     Key changes: evolved from `\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}` → 10 → 12 → 4 → 6. Removed `\vspace{-4mm}` entirely. Practitioner framework paragraph compressed multiple times for page fit.

   - **solutions.tex** — §6 Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept
     - DRC paragraph compressed from 3 lines to 1-line appendix reference: `A cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\tau$, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}$35\% NLI cost ($\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\ref{app:drc}).`
     - Capability gap: removed Spearman ρ=1.0, p=0.003 detail, kept figure/appendix refs

   - **appendix.tex** — Multiple additions:
     - Extended Discussion (after line ~1110): Added 3 new \paragraph sections:
       1. "SCB diagnostic toolkit" (app:diagnostic-toolkit) — BR/K_eff/C₀/ProcessSim calculation methods, thresholds, decision rules
       2. "Future directions: trained extractors and beyond" (app:future-directions) — trained extractors, multi-model ensembles, external knowledge, SCB-aware aggregation
       3. "SCB as an evaluation framework" (app:scb-evaluation) — proposing BR/K_eff/CCR as standard metrics
     - "Implications for neuro-symbolic practitioners" rewritten to remove overlap with main Discussion
     - DRC section: added explanatory text before existing table
     - Multi-seed table added as \paragraph within Section Y (Single-Trace and Aggregation Statistics), before Sub-population Analysis:
       ```latex
       \paragraph{Multi-seed replication.}
       \label{app:multi-seed}
       \begin{table}[h]
       \centering\small
       \caption{Multi-seed replication results...}
       \label{tab:multi-seed}
       \begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrl@{}}
       ...Qwen2.5-14B × FOLIO, seeds 42/123/456/789, Mean±SD...
       \end{tabular}
       \end{table}
       ```

   - **related_work.tex** — Two citation additions:
     - CJT paragraph: "Classical ensemble diversity theory (Kuncheva and Whitaker, 2003; Brown et al., 2005) formalizes this intuition: diversity measures (e.g., κ-statistic, disagreement) predict ensemble gain. Our K_eff adapts Fleiss' κ for this purpose."
     - Test-time compute paragraph: added `\citet{valmeekam2023llms} further show fundamental planning limitations that compound self-verification failures.`

   - **references.bib** — Added 3 entries:
     - `valmeekam2023llms` (NeurIPS 2023, planning abilities)
     - `kuncheva2003measures` (Machine Learning 2003, diversity measures)
     - (brown2005diversity was already present, just needed citation in .tex)

   - **experiments.tex** — Two changes:
     - Line 46: Added `$^{4s}

 marker to Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO row in Table 1
     - After Mistral four-seed sentence: Added "Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B × FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp (mean $-1.24 \pm 0.82$; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed})."
     - Table 1 caption already had `$^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory` notation

   - **limitations_content.tex** — Updated single-seed mention:
     - Old: "most conditions use a single seed (four-seed Δ range spans ~4 pp)."
     - New: "most conditions use a single seed; four-seed replication on Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO confirms the single-seed patterns (Δ range spans ~4 pp and ~2 pp, respectively; Table ref), with multi-seed data for remaining conditions in progress."

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Undefined `eq:br` reference**: Referenced `\ref{eq:br}` in appendix SCB diagnostic toolkit, but no such equation label exists. Fixed by changing to `\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}`.
   - **Undefined `wang2023selfconsistency` citation**: Used wrong bib key in appendix SCB evaluation framework. Correct key is `wang2023sc`. Fixed by replacing.
   - **Page 8 overflow (recurring)**: Multiple iterations needed to fit Discussion on page 8:
     - `\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}` → 10 (fixed original overflow)
     - External modification of Practitioner framework paragraph added text → needed 12
     - Director requested reducing to 4 → too aggressive, still overflowed
     - Tried 6 + `\vspace{-1mm}` → still overflowed
     - Tried 6 + no vspace + text compression → worked
     - Key compressions: Practitioner framework ("In our experiments" removed, compact $\Delta \in [0.00, -0.98]$ notation), Capability gap (Spearman detail removed)
   - **System reminder false positives**: Multiple "[系统提醒]...没有调用 main_agent_post_to_director" messages. Correctly ignored — Paper Agent uses `paper_agent_post_to_director`, not `main_agent_post_to_director`.

5. Problem Solving:
   - Page 8 fitting problem solved through iterative approach: DRC move to appendix freed ~2 lines, text compression freed ~2 lines, settled on `\enlargethispage{6\baselineskip}` as a reasonable middle ground
   - Appendix section limit (26 = A-Z) solved by using \paragraph within existing sections instead of new \section
   - Related Work gap analysis: identified missing ensemble diversity and planning limitations citations, verified formal verification coverage was already sufficient
   - Appendix dedup: identified and resolved overlap between "Implications for neuro-symbolic practitioners" and main Discussion

        "content": "\\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals---whether self-extraction on frontier models would yield different BR values remains untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. NLI verification was tested only on propositional/first-order logic tasks; generalization to non-logical reasoning domains remains untested. Syntactic deduplication (46.0\\% removal rate) may retain semantically equivalent constraints; however, the shuffle control (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) preserves the same duplication pattern and yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{shuffle} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$, suggesting deduplication granularity does not drive the observed bias direction. McNemar power is below 21\\% for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed; four-seed replication on Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO confirms the single-seed patterns ($\\Delta$ range spans ${\\sim}4$\\,pp and ${\\sim}2$\\,pp, respectively; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}), with multi-seed data for remaining conditions in progress. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions sharing verifier components ($p = 0.167$ after collapsing); we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. SICA requires $4.5\\times$ the wall-clock time of SC with no significant accuracy gain. Extended limitations are discussed in Appendix~\\ref{app:limitations-extended}.\n",
        "numLines": 2,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 2
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/limitations_content.tex"

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four Mistral seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.82$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 75
      }

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\enlargethispage{6\\baselineskip}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: in our experiments, no condition improves ($\\Delta \\in [0.00, -0.98]$\\,pp across four conditions; Table~\\ref{tab:independence}). When SC falls below ${\\sim}40\\%$, constraints amplify errors ($-2.83$\\,pp on Mistral-7B ProofWriter). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces reduce any aggregation to majority voting by construction. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",
  "uuid": "8b3d74fe-1e36-4bda-8f78-211e768c788a",

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/solutions.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded as design principles in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",
        "numLines": 36,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 36
      }

1050	
1051	Controlling for the trace set (excluding $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$, which uses a different seed), all conditions yield $\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp (n.s.), indicating that extraction temperature has no significant effect on SICA accuracy. This supports the structural nature of symbolic confirmation bias: the bottleneck is what gets extracted, not the temperature at which extraction occurs.
1052	
1053	\paragraph{Multi-seed replication.}
1054	\label{app:multi-seed}

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

66	\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four Mistral seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp (mean $-1.24 \pm 0.82$; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\,pp at 80\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\pm 1$\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\pm 3$\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\pm 2$\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\,pp \citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.

We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four Mistral seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.82$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.",

66	\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four Mistral seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp (mean $-1.24 \pm 0.86$; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\,pp at 80\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\pm 1$\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\pm 3$\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\pm 2$\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\,pp \citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.

1	\looseness=-1 Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters with self-extraction; frontier models confirm SCB universality but use cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B, as closed-source APIs do not expose internals---whether self-extraction on frontier models would yield different BR values remains untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks; we do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, multilingual settings, or commonsense domains lacking gold annotations. NLI verification was tested only on propositional/first-order logic tasks; generalization to non-logical reasoning domains remains untested. Syntactic deduplication (46.0\% removal rate) may retain semantically equivalent constraints; however, the shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) preserves the same duplication pattern and yields $\text{BR}_\text{shuffle} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$, suggesting deduplication granularity does not drive the observed bias direction. McNemar power is below 21\% for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp at $n{=}200$--$600$, meaning small true effects may go undetected; most conditions use a single seed; four-seed replication on Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO confirms the single-seed patterns ($\Delta$ range spans ${\sim}4$\,pp and ${\sim}2$\,pp, respectively; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}), with multi-seed data for remaining conditions in progress. The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ conditions sharing verifier components ($p = 0.167$ after collapsing); we characterize it as a consistent pattern rather than a confirmed law. SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC with no significant accuracy gain. Extended limitations are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.
2

Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

66	\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \pm 2.94$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp (mean $-1.24 \pm 0.86$; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\,pp at 80\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\pm 1$\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\pm 3$\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\pm 2$\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\,pp \citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.

The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.94$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes.     "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.94$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.94$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0113r9aSABwkuAMiViLWE8VL`</summary>

```text
110	SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime (Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter}).
111	
112	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--55\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect.
113	
114	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%).} All nine Type~A conditions produce no significant positive effects. Even in-context learning with gold logical examples yields $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp (n.s.). Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$).
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch — R14 审稿修复，高优先级]\n\nEMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL May 25。Round 14 回来 3.5，目标 4.0。以下是经 Reviewer 独立评估确认的修改清单，按优先级执行。引用不需要验证（Agent 正在 WebSearch 确认）。\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix (Critical)\n\n### 1. 标题限定（R14-N1）\n- 原：Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints Show No Evidence of Improving LLM Reasoning\n- 改：**Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Constraints Fail to Improve LLM Logical Reasoning**\n- 同步修改 Abstract/Intro/Conclusion 中所有 \"LLM reasoning\" → \"LLM logical reasoning\" 或 \"logical reasoning\"。注意不要过度修改——当讨论 general implications 时 \"reasoning\" 仍然合适。\n\n### 2. §3.3 Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis 重构（R14-N2）\n- 当前 §3.3 说 \"Three testable predictions follow\"，但 Prediction 1 (BR>1) 和 Prediction 2 (no remediation) 实际是事后总结\n- 重构方案：\n  - 保留 \"Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis\" 名称（hypothesis 有因果链理论价值）\n  - 将 Prediction 1-2 重定位为 \"Motivating Observations\"（在初始模型上发现，后在 9 模型上验证的经验规律）\n  - 仅 Prediction 3 (cross-architecture escape) 保留为 \"Testable Prediction\"\n  - 措辞示例：\"Two observations motivate the hypothesis: (O1) BR > 1 across all tested self-extraction conditions; (O2) no training-free remediation reduces BR below 1. - 同步修改 Abstract/Intro/Conclusion 中所有 "LLM reasoning" → "LLM logical reasoning" 或 "logical reasoning"。注意不要过度修改——当讨论 general implications 时 "reasoning" 仍然合适。

### 2. §3.3 Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis 重构（R14-N2）
- 当前 §3.3 说 "Three testable predictions follow"，但 Prediction 1 (BR>1) 和 Prediction 2 (no remediation) 实际是事后总结
- 重构方案：

- **同步修改 analysis.tex §5 末尾验证段落**（当前说 "The three predictions of §3.3 are confirmed"）→ 改为区分 "confirmed observations" vs "validated prediction"

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01JBicEV4zZRtqexwnWnA4kH`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$5\,pp.
4	We trace the failure to \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios ranging from $1.66\times$ to $4.97\times$), but a shuffle control shows this bias is structural---it reflects trace-count distributions rather than extraction-specific error ($\text{BR}_\text{obs} \approx \text{BR}_\text{null}$, $p > 0.7$). MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems, confirming that the formal verification layer adds no information beyond majority voting despite 99\% of constraints passing Z3 validation.
5	Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$), though frontier models use cross-model rather than self-extraction.
6	Twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, confirming a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off.
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).
9	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013dcmiZ8JdUBzF7oBsdbJ5p`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This independence limitation motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\pm}0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in [0, 1]$).
45	
46	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
47	\label{sec:mirage}
48	
49	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
50	\begin{equation}
51	\label{eq:mirage}
52	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
53	\end{equation}
54	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
55	
56	\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]
57	\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\label{prop:ceiling}
58	For generator $M$ with $\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\Delta_\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\Delta_\text{oracle} / \text{BR}(M)$.
59	\end{tcolorbox}
60	
61	\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]
62	\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\label{cor:capability_gap}
63	Let $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.
64	\end{tcolorbox}
65	
66	\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\text{eff}^\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
67	
68	We also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.
69	
```

The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nWe also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.\n",
      "numLines": 69,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 69
    }
  },
